This guards\n23\t  against typos in the static `CAPABILITIES` table that COMP-009 will\n24\t  declare in T01.11.\n25\t- Two instances built from identical arguments compare equal via the\n26\t  `@dataclass`-generated `__eq__`.\n27\t- The `check` callable is **not** invoked at construction time. claude` empty) | Abort run with exit code 2. |\n37\t| `skip` | `check()` returns `False`, **or** the user passes the descriptor's `skip_flag` (e.g. `--no-mcp`) | Mark gated evals SKIPPED with reason; continue. |\n38\t| `xfail` | Manifest declares `xfail_if:` and condition matched | Run, but expected-fail (status becomes XPASS if it passes anyway). |\n39\t\n40\t## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n41\t\n42\t- COMP-009 `CapabilityGates` (T01.11) — instantiates a static\n43\t  `CAPABILITIES` list of these descriptors and iterates them in\n44\t  `check_all()`.\n45\t- `CapabilityReport` the 5 fields. | `capabilities.py` — `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Capability` |\n55\t| Instantiation with an invalid `failure_mode` raises `ValueError`. | `Capability.__post_init__` (covered by `test_capability_rejects_invalid_failure_modes`). |\n56\t| Two instances built from the same arguments compare equal. | `@dataclass`-generated `__eq__` (covered by continue. |\n| `xfail` | Manifest declares `xfail_if:` and condition matched | Run, but expected-fail (status becomes XPASS if it passes anyway). |\n\n## Caller contract (downstream consumers)\n\n- COMP-009 `CapabilityGates` (T01.11) — instantiates a static\n  `CAPABILITIES` list of these descriptors and iterates them in\n  `check_all()`.\n- `CapabilityReport` (T01.10) — collects PASS/FAIL per `Capability.name`\n  and groups blocked evals by `failure_mode`.\n- `eval doctor` (T01.13) — renders the green checklist using\n  `description` and `failure_mode`.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T01.09) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| Class `Capability` is a frozen dataclass exposing the 5 fields. | `capabilities.py` — `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Capability` |\n| Instantiation with an invalid `failure_mode` raises `ValueError`. | `Capability.__post_init__` (covered by `test_capability_rejects_invalid_failure_modes`). |\n| Two instances built from the same arguments compare equal. | `@dataclass`-generated `__eq__` (covered by `test_capability_deterministic_equality`). |\n| `spec.md` records the 5-field contract. | This file. |\n\n## Out of scope for T01.09\n\n- The static `CAPABILITIES` table (`binary.claude`, `binary.jq`,\n  `mcp_server.auggie`, …) — lives in COMP-009 (T01.11).\n- `CapabilityReport` aggregate model — T01.10 (DM-008).\n- `which_or_skip` / `mcp_server_reachable` implementations — T01.11.\n- Wiring of `--no-mcp` CLI flag — T01.13.\n","numLines":66,"startLine":1,"totalLines":66}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01G7UwEvL7M3QVddamDfo4eV","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Capability dataclasses for the cliEval harness.\n2\t\n3\t* ``Capability``        — DM-007 / D-0008 (Task T01.09): descriptor for a\n4\t  single precondition the harness checks before running evals.\n5\t* ``CapabilityStatus``  — DM-008 helper: per-capability outcome carried in\n6\t  ``CapabilityReport.report``.\n7\t* ``CapabilityReport``  — DM-008 / D-0009 (Task T01.10): aggregate report\n8\t  produced by COMP-009 ``CapabilityGates.check_all`` and consumed by\n9\t  ``eval doctor`` (T01.13) and the run pre-flight.\n10\t\n11\t``Capability`` 5-field contract (DM-007):\n12\t\n13\t* ``name``         — capability identifier (e.g. ``binary.claude``).\n14\t* ``check``        — zero-arg callable returning ``True`` when the\n15\t  capability is satisfied. The callable is evaluated by\n16\t  ``CapabilityGates.check_all`` (not at descriptor construction time).\n17\t* ``failure_mode`` — one of ``\"hard\"``, ``\"skip\"``, ``\"xfail\"``. ``hard``\n18\t  aborts the run; ``skip`` marks gated evals as ``SKIPPED``; ``xfail``\n19\t  marks them as expected-fail (see design-spec §11 \"Three tiers of gates\").\n20\t* ``skip_flag``    — optional CLI flag (e.g. ``--no-mcp``) that forces\n21\t  soft-skip behaviour even when the check would otherwise pass.\n22\t* ``description``  — human-readable label for the doctor output.\n23\t\n24\t``CapabilityReport`` 6-field contract (DM-008):\n25\t\n26\t* ``report``         — tuple of ``CapabilityStatus`` entries, one per\n27\t  capability that was evaluated.\n28\t* ``blocked_evals``  — eval ids gated off by a failing or skip-flagged\n29\t  capability.\n30\t* ``skip_flags``     — CLI flags currently active (e.g. ``(\"--no-mcp\",)``).\n31\t* ``hard_failures``  — names of capabilities classified ``hard`` that\n32\t  failed; \"git\", \"binary\", \"hard\", None,\n        \"git VCS on PATH\",\n    ),\n    _CapabilitySpec(\n        \"mcp_server.auggie\", \"auggie\", \"mcp_server\", \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\",\n        \"Auggie MCP server reachable\",\n    ),\n    _CapabilitySpec(\n        \"mcp_server.auggie-mcp\", \"auggie-mcp\", \"mcp_server\", \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\",\n        \"auggie-mcp MCP server reachable\",\n    ),\n    _CapabilitySpec(\n        \"mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway\", \"airis-mcp-gateway\",\n        \"mcp_server\", \"skip\", \"--no-mcp\",\n        \"AIRIS MCP gateway reachable\",\n    ),\n)\n\n\n# Type alias for the (passed, detail) tuple returned by per-target probes.\nProbeResult = tuple[bool, str]\n\n\nclass CapabilityGates:\n    \"\"\"COMP-009: evaluate every capability and emit a ``CapabilityReport``.\n\n    The gate is constructed once per ``eval doctor`` / ``eval run``\n    invocation. Pass the active CLI ``skip_flags`` (e.g. ``{\"--no-mcp\"}``)\n    so SOFT-SKIP capabilities whose descriptor names a matching flag are\n    forced to skipped status even when their underlying probe would pass.\n\n    The class is purposely small: ``check_all`` drives the static\n    :data:`_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS` roster through :meth:`which_or_skip`\n    or :meth:`mcp_server_reachable` and assembles the report. Tests\n    inject a custom spec tuple via the ``capabilities`` keyword to mock\n    PATH state without monkeypatching ``shutil``.\n\n    OQ-5 (roadmap Open Questions): the precise ``mcp_server_reachable``\n    contract (PATH presence vs handshake vs SSE probe) must be resolved\n    before COMP-009 close at M2. The M1 implementation here uses PATH\n    presence as the reachability signal — see :meth:`mcp_server_reachable`\n    for the deferral note and the override hook.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,\n        capabilities: Optional[Iterable[_CapabilitySpec]] = None,\n        mcp_probe: Optional[Callable[[str], ProbeResult]] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        self._skip_flags: frozenset[str] = frozenset(skip_flags or ())\n        self._specs: tuple[_CapabilitySpec, ...] = tuple(\n            capabilities if capabilities is not None else _DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS\n        )\n        # Test hook so ``mcp_server_reachable`` can be redirected without\n        # monkeypatching the module. Production callers leave this ``None``\n        # and the default PATH-presence probe (OQ-5 M1 stub) is used.\n        self._mcp_probe = mcp_probe\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # public API\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @property\n    def skip_flags(self) -> tuple[str, ...]:\n        \"\"\"Active CLI skip flags, sorted for deterministic report output.\"\"\"\n        return tuple(sorted(self._skip_flags))\n\n    def capabilities(self) -> tuple[Capability, ...]:\n        \"\"\"Materialise the static spec table as ``Capability`` instances.\n\n        Each ``Capability.check`` is a closure that defers to\n        :meth:`which_or_skip` / :meth:`mcp_server_reachable` so the\n        descriptors stay in sync with the gate's own probing — there is no\n        second copy of the lookup logic in the closures.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return tuple(self._make_capability(spec) for spec in self._specs)\n\n    def which_or_skip(self, name: str) -> ProbeResult:\n        \"\"\"Probe whether binary ``name`` is on ``PATH``.\n\n        Returns ``(True, \"<resolved-path>\")`` when ``shutil.which`` finds\n        the binary, ``(False, \"not found on PATH\")`` otherwise. The\n        ``detail`` half is carried verbatim onto the\n        :class:`CapabilityStatus` row so ``eval doctor`` can print the\n        resolved path on success or the failure reason on miss.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        path = shutil.which(name)\n        if path is None:\n            return False, \"not found on PATH\"\n        return True, path\n\n    def mcp_server_reachable(self, name: str) -> ProbeResult:\n        \"\"\"Probe whether MCP server ``name`` is reachable.\n\n        OQ-5 deferral: the canonical reachability semantics (PATH\n        presence vs full stdio handshake vs SSE health probe) are open\n        and must resolve before COMP-009 closes at M2. Until then, this\n        implementation treats \"binary on PATH\" as the reachability\n        signal because every MCP server in the default roster\n        (``auggie``, ``auggie-mcp``, ``airis-mcp-gateway``) ships as an\n        executable.\n\n        Tests inject a custom probe via the ``mcp_probe`` constructor\n        argument; the M2 follow-up will land a real handshake probe\n        under the same signature.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._mcp_probe is not None:\n            return self._mcp_probe(name)\n        path = shutil.which(name)\n        if path is None:\n            return False, \"MCP server binary not on PATH\"\n        return True, path\n\n    def check_all(self) -> CapabilityReport:\n        \"\"\"Run every spec through its probe and assemble the report.\n\n        Returns:\n            A :class:`CapabilityReport` whose ``report`` tuple has one\n            :class:`CapabilityStatus` row per spec in declaration order.\n            ``hard_failures`` / ``soft_skips`` / ``soft_xfails`` carry\n            the capability names that failed (or were skip-flagged) per\n            their declared ``failure_mode``. ``blocked_evals`` is empty\n            here — the SuiteLoader (T01.07) is the layer that knows\n            which evals depend on which capability and populates that\n            list at run time.\n\n            Calling :meth:`check_all` twice on the same gate instance\n            produces equal reports (idempotence is required by the\n            T01.11 acceptance criteria; verified by\n            ``test_check_all_is_idempotent``).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        rows: list[CapabilityStatus] = []\n        hard_failures: list[str] = []\n        soft_skips: list[str] = []\n        soft_xfails: list[str] = []\n\n        for spec in self._specs:\n            passed, detail = self._probe(spec)\n            skipped_by_flag = False\n            if (\n                spec.skip_flag is not None\n                and spec.skip_flag in self._skip_flags\n            ):\n                # SOFT-SKIP override: the user asked us to ignore this\n                # capability tier regardless of probe result. The probe\n                # detail is preserved so doctor output can still show\n                # why the capability would have passed/failed without\n                # the flag.\n                skipped_by_flag = True\n                passed = False\n\n            rows.append(\n                CapabilityStatus(\n                    name=spec.name,\n                    passed=passed,\n                    failure_mode=spec.failure_mode,\n                    description=spec.description,\n                    detail=detail,\n                    skipped_by_flag=skipped_by_flag,\n                )\n            )\n\n            if not passed:\n                if spec.failure_mode == \"hard\":\n                    hard_failures.append(spec.name)\n                elif spec.failure_mode == \"skip\":\n                    soft_skips.append(spec.name)\n                else:  # \"xfail\"\n                    soft_xfails.append(spec.name)\n\n        return CapabilityReport(\n            report=tuple(rows),\n            blocked_evals=(),\n            skip_flags=self.skip_flags,\n            hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n            soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n            soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n        )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _probe(self, spec: _CapabilitySpec) -> ProbeResult:\n        if spec.kind == \"binary\":\n            return self.which_or_skip(spec.target)\n        if spec.kind == \"mcp_server\":\n            return self.mcp_server_reachable(spec.target)\n        # Defensive — the Literal annotation prevents this at type-check\n        # time, but the runtime guard keeps a typo in a custom spec\n        # tuple from silently producing an always-passing row.\n        raise ValueError(f\"unknown capability kind: {spec.kind!r}\")\n\n    def _make_capability(self, spec: _CapabilitySpec) -> Capability:\n        if spec.kind == \"binary\":\n            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:\n                return self.which_or_skip(target)[0]\n        else:\n            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:\n                return self.mcp_server_reachable(target)[0]\n        return Capability(\n            name=spec.name,\n            check=_check,\n            failure_mode=spec.failure_mode,\n            skip_flag=spec.skip_flag,\n            description=spec.description,\n        )\n","numLines":410,"startLine":1,"totalLines":410}}}

Their interior shape is validated by ``suite.schema.json``;\n      richer typed models land later (ExpectCallable in M4).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    id: str\n    title: str\n    category: str = \"\"\n    requires: tuple[str, ...] = ()\n    timeout_sec: int | None = None\n    isolation: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None\n    inputs: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n    expects: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n    parameterize: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_dict(cls, data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> \"EvalSpec\":\n        \"\"\"Build an ``EvalSpec`` from a schema-valid manifest entry.\n\n        The mapping keys mirror ``suite.schema.json`` field names. Sequence\n        values (``requires``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``) are\n        normalised to tuples; nested mappings inside ``inputs`` / ``expects``\n        / ``parameterize`` rows are passed through as-is (the schema already\n        constrained their shape).\n\n        Missing optional keys fall back to the dataclass defaults; ``id`` and\n        ``title`` are required by the schema upstream so absence here is a\n        contract bug rather than user error.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if \"id\" not in data or \"title\" not in data:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"EvalSpec.from_dict requires schema-validated input with 'id' and 'title'\"\n            )\n\n        requires_raw = data.get(\"requires\", ())\n        inputs_raw = data.get(\"inputs\", ())\n        expects_raw = data.get(\"expects\", ())\n        parameterize_raw = data.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n\n        return cls(\n            id=data[\"id\"],\n            title=data[\"title\"],\n            category=data.get(\"category\", \"\"),\n            requires=tuple(requires_raw),\n            timeout_sec=data.get(\"timeout_sec\"),\n            isolation=data.get(\"isolation\"),\n            inputs=tuple(inputs_raw),\n            expects=tuple(expects_raw),\n            parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),\n        )\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":98,"oldLines":3,"newStart":98,"newLines":60,"lines":["             expects=tuple(expects_raw),","             parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),","         )","+","+","+@dataclass(frozen=True)","+class ExpectResult:","+    \"\"\"Assertion outcome returned by every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-009).","+","+    Six-field contract:","+","+    * ``name``         — assertion name, matching the DSL method that emitted","+      the result (e.g. ``\"exit_code\"``, ``\"file.contains_event\"``). Used by","+      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.","+    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The","+      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.","+    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal","+      summary; empty string when no message is required.","+    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the","+      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON","+      report. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass","+      keeps its equality / hashability contract; an empty mapping is the","+      default for passing results.","+    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds the assertion took to evaluate.","+      Stored as ``float`` to capture sub-millisecond resolution.","+    * ``failure``      — optional ``ExpectFailure`` (DM-005 / T01.16) carrying","+      the diff-level detail for a failing assertion. ``None`` for passing","+      results; DM-009 does not couple the failure presence to ``passed``, so","+      callers must populate ``failure`` explicitly when they want the","+      reporter to render a rich per-Expect failure block.","+","+    Equality is structural across all six fields (``@dataclass``-generated","+    ``__eq__``). The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``","+    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.","+    \"\"\"","+","+    name: str","+    passed: bool","+    message: str = \"\"","+    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)","+    duration_sec: float = 0.0","+    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None","+","+    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:","+        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.","+","+        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably","+        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into","+        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared","+        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the","+        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.","+        \"\"\"","+","+        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)","+        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may","+        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).","+        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work","+        # needed here. Resolution: declare the field as `Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"]` (string forward annotation) — `from __future__ import annotations` (already in the module) makes the annotation a runtime string, so the module imports cleanly today. T01.16 will add the concrete class to the same module and the annotation resolves automatically. No production-code edit needed when T01.16 lands.\n\n3. **`details` defaults to `field(default_factory=dict)`.** `Mapping[str, Any]` is the static type so callers can pass any mapping-shaped payload (dict, MappingProxyType, etc.). `default_factory=dict` gives each instance its own empty mapping — verified by `test_expect_result_details_default_is_independent_per_instance` so a shared mutable default cannot leak between results.\n\n4. **`to_dict()` delegates to `dataclasses.asdict`.** Two reasons: (a) DM-009 explicitly names `dataclasses.asdict` as the serialisation surface in the task acceptance criteria, and (b) `asdict` recursively unwraps nested dataclasses, so once T01.16's `ExpectFailure` is set on `failure`, the Reporter (COMP-008) gets a plain dict without writing bespoke handling. Locked by `test_expect_result_asdict_unwraps_nested_dataclass_failure`.\n\n5. **`duration_sec: float = 0.0`.** Acceptance criterion uses `duration_sec` (not the DM-009 short name `duration`); chose `float` so sub-millisecond assertion timings are not truncated. The default `0.0` lets unit tests construct passing results without supplying timing data.\n\n6. **`failure` is Optional with no required-when-failed coupling.** DM-009 wording is explicit: \"failure is Optional per DM-009 (no required-when-failed coupling)\". Implementation matches: `passed=False, failure=None` constructs successfully (`test_expect_result_failing_without_failure_attached_is_allowed`). The Reporter is responsible for treating absence-of-failure on a failing result as \"no rich diff available\", not as a contract violation.\n\n7. **Test using a dataclass stand-in instead of an `ExpectFailure` mock.** Since T01.16 is not yet landed, the failing-with-failure tests construct a local `@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)` stand-in and pass it as the `failure` value. This proves `to_dict()` recurses correctly without preempting T01.16's 8-field shape. When T01.16 lands, a follow-up test can swap in the real type — the contract this test locks (asdict recursion) does not change.\n\n8. **`__init__.py` re-export.** Added `ExpectResult` to the package `__all__` so downstream callers can `from superclaude.cli.eval import ExpectResult` symmetric to `EvalSpec`. Both names ship from the same module.\n\n## Things deliberately NOT in scope of T01.15\n\n- `ExpectFailure` dataclass — DM-005 / T01.16 (separate task).\n- Replacing the test stand-in with the real `ExpectFailure` import — defer to T01.16 close-out.\n- ExpectCallable primitives that actually populate `ExpectResult` fields — M4 (COMP-010.1–6).\n- Reporter integration (`to_dict()` consumer) — COMP-008 / T03.13.\n- `eval doctor --json` payload schema update — T01.13 already emits the CapabilityReport; ExpectResult enters the picture only via `eval run` (M3).\n\n## Risks observed during build\n\n- **Forward-reference fragility.** If a future refactor removes `from __future__ import annotations` from `models.py`, the `Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"]` annotation will fail at import time when ExpectFailure isn't defined yet. Mitigation: T01.16 lands ExpectFailure in the same module before any code path actually evaluates the annotation, and the module docstring + this notes file flag the dependency.\n- **`Mapping[str, Any]` with `default_factory=dict`.** The static type is broader than the runtime default; that is intentional so callers may pass `MappingProxyType` if they want deep immutability. No tooling complains today but a future strict-typing pass may want to tighten this.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2085,"cache_read_input_tokens":122943,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2085,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":46,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e8eccfe0-9949-41e8-b4c9-939ff2ce12b1","uuid":"9a5b8eaa-339c-43a0-9b1c-0d0170cd1dba"}
